Identifiability and dependence structure in factor models through monomial assignments in determinantal decompositions
Mario Angelelli

Abstract
Motivated by the investigation of algebraic approaches to study identifiability and ambiguity in factor modelling, we introduce a method to explore the properties of a factor of interest, in particular its dependence structure and sensitivity to deformations, under partial observability described by a pattern matrix. The proposal is based on deformations of matrix factorisations under the condition that the terms of their determinantal decompositions are monomials. We identify a trivial class of monomial assignments compatible with determinantal constraints and find minimal conditions on the pattern matrix, which correspond to forbidden configurations for graph planarity, guaranteeing that only such trivial assignments are valid. Counterexamples are provided in which these conditions do not hold. This allows us to connect with relevant notions for reasoning about model uncertainty, including factor indeterminacy, non-additivity of set functions, and the application of the counterexamples to represent non-Bayesian preferences in choice theory.

Our aim is to investigate minimal conditions on the independence structure defined by the set (
1.2
) that ensure that the trivial choices generating the reduction (
1.5
)
are the only valid ones. We will refer to this situation as the
rigidity
of the expansion (
1.1
).

This problem turns out to be related to different notions for the study of model uncertainty
in factor analysis, and it returns an algebraic representation of deviations from rational choice axioms. With the aim of providing a theoretical background for these connections, we will contextualise the proposed framework
in relation to methods that examine structural relations
[
10
]
and factor indeterminacy in multivariate models
[
35
,
33
]
. The proofs to demonstrate the main technical results of this work (Theorems
11
and
28
) guide us in the construction of counterexamples where the information content provided by
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
does not suffice to ensure (
1.5
). This elucidates the minimality of the identified conditions with respect to the set-theoretic inclusion between subsets of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
. The counterexamples are combined with a ranking criterion to generate deviations from the Independence of Irrelevant Alternatives axiom
[
28
]
, i.e. the lack of an invariance property implied by the Bayesian paradigm.

The rest of the paper is organised as follows: in Section
2
, we discuss the premises, set the main notation adopted throughout the paper, and formalise the conditions entailing the rigidity property. In Section
3
, we present the main results of this work in relation to methods for dealing with factor indeterminacy (Subsection
3.1
) and ambiguity in preferences (Subsection
3.2
). In Section
4
, after the statement of preliminary lemmas, we analyse in detail the effect of the monomial constraint (
1.3
). In Section
5
, the results of this analysis let us identify a basis in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
satisfying a precondition for the rigidity property; in our notation, we refer to such a base as
integrable
. In Section
6
, we show how the integrability property is transferred among bases under weaker assumptions than those considered in the previous sections. In Section
7
, counterexamples based on deviations from the assumptions are illustrated in connection with the lack of Independence of Irrelevant Alternatives, and we present a Mathematica code for their symbolic verification in Appendix
A
. Finally, we draw conclusions and discuss future work in Section
8
.

2
Preliminaries

2.1
Relation to previous work

This paper continues the research started in
[
3
]
and extended in
[
4
]
to explore the information-theoretic properties of determinantal deformations and their potential applications, with special regard to complexity reduction in combinatorial systems. The first work
[
3
]
was motivated by the use of a special class of solitary waves
[
24
]
to encode information about sign configurations. These waves can be expressed as a combination of exponentials using (
1.1
); such a parametrisation is central to the analysis of the asymptotic behaviour of the waves (specifically, their tropical limit
[
24
,
3
]
), and a logarithmic transformation of variables makes each term in (
1.1
) a monomial, in line with (
1.3
).

The second work
[
4
]
proposes determinantal expansions to encode information about set permutations. Indeed, any permutation of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
can be encoded as a
\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\}^{\mathchar 29038}
-valued set function; then, the results of
[
4
]
at
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29038
allow identifying a permutation of
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
that generates the original set permutation, if it exists. This approach is suitable for applications involving uncertain labelling, specifically linear regression with shuffled data
[
31
]
and unlabelled sensing
[
38
]
.

These investigations, along with the study of order inequivalence between different physical descriptions of a system
[
2
]
, are linked to the present work through the focus on the labelling and identity of certain objects of interest (subsystems labelled by their ground energy in
[
2
]
, elements of
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
labelling vectors here and in
[
3
,
4
]
). These objects exist within multiple collections whose compatibility is determined by algebraic conditions. This motivates the attention paid to identifiability and ambiguity, which we deepen in Section
3
.

Example 3
.

\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathrm{\mathchar 29029\mathchar 29048}}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{\mathchar29040\mathchar29037\mathchar29025\mathchar29044\mathchar29042\mathchar29033\mathchar29048}\mathchar 28721&\mathchar 28720&\mathchar 28720&\mathchar 28720&\mathchar 28721&\mathchar 28721&\mathchar 28723\\

(2.9)

\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 28720\}

Remark 4
.

Example
3
elucidates the naming “(weak) key”: the properties of the key

obtained from

Definition 8

A key

is said to be

\mathcal{\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29027}_{\mathchar 29027}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 8814\mathfrak{\mathchar 29027}_{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathcal{\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 67273472\mathfrak{\mathchar 29027}_{\mathchar 29042}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 28995}}\mathchar 8814\mathfrak{\mathchar 29027}_{\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 314

Remark 9
.

We choose the attribute “non-planar” since there is a correspondence between a non-planar key and one of the graphs
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28723\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723}
or
\mathchar 29003_{\mathchar 28725}
characterising the obstruction to graph planarity in Kuratowski’s theorem (see e.g.
[
30
, Thm. 2.3.8]
). To show this, we fix a non-planar key
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29027}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28722}\}\mathchar 8706\{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28722}\}
and take an index, say
\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
, such that
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}}^{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
.

First assume that
\mathchar 28720\mathchar 12850\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28722}}^{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29045}}\delimiter 84054785
for some
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84267779
with
\mathchar 29043\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 29045
, say
\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
where

3
Main contribution and its applications

Our analysis identifies a local and structural property that allows recovering the global rigidity, i.e. Assumption
10
: this condition is local since it refers to a submatrix of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
and is independent of the full structure
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
; it is structural since it is independent of both the set function
\mathchar 28681
in (
2.7
) and the values of non-vanishing entries of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
.
This is formalised in Theorem
11
stated below, which will be proved through a more general result (Theorem
28
).

Theorem 11
.

Before moving to the scrutiny and proofs of the formal properties related to this result, we devote the rest of the section to the discussion of its connections with methods and notions to deal with specific types of uncertainty in structural modelling and choice theory.

3.1
Relation to structural modelling under partial information and factor indeterminacy

Factor analysis aims at modelling the relations between a set of observable variables and a set of underlying (often unobservable or latent) factors
[
29
]
. In different multivariate analyses, the solutions of a common factor model are based on the observed covariance matrix of manifest variables. However, such models are affected by non-identifiability issues, namely, one cannot uniquely reconstruct, starting from observed data and factor patterns, the common and unique factors, which constitute the sources of the common variance explained by the model and the unique variance, respectively
[
35
]
. Such factor indeterminacy represents a critical aspect of model uncertainty affecting the analysis of conceptual constructs (see e.g.
[
33
]
and references therein).

While (confirmatory) factor analysis aims at assessing a measurement model linking manifest variables and latent constructs or factors, structural equation modelling
[
9
]
also takes into account relations among such constructs. Linear structural equation modelling based on observed covariance is one of the most common approaches in the field of causal modelling
[
32
]
.

Matrix minors play a relevant role in factor analysis and linear structural equation modelling. First, minors of a correlation matrix are used to express partial correlations and apply them in the study of factor indeterminacy, considering external variables to test the underdetermined model
[
35
]
in terms of correlation uncertainty (also see
[
33
, Eqs. 8–10]
). In the broader context of structural equation modelling, minors of correlation or covariance matrices are used in the
tetrad
approach to test the occurrence of causal (or formative) indicators within a graphical model
[
10
]
. This approach has been extended to obtain test statistics based on algebraic invariants
[
17
]
and to graphically characterise the vanishing minors
[
37
,
16
]
. Ratios of minors of the covariance matrix also serve as a base for a generalisation of instrumental variables (see e.g.
[
11
]
, where partial correlations are discussed too), supporting the study of the identifiability of structural models
[
39
]
.

The excluded value
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
for
\mathchar 29017
-terms at generic
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}
(Subsection
2.3
) corresponds to the vanishing of a
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
-minor of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
, which makes the ratios (
2.12
) the analogue of tetrads in our context. This indicates a connection between the aforementioned methodologies and the present work, where we include a notion of observability based on the three-term Grassmann-Plücker relations and Definition
2
. To clarify this connection, we first address the role of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}
in selecting observable terms. The independence structure
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
acts as a filter that lets us access partial information on relations satisfied by
\mathchar 29017
-terms. In this way, we exploit all information from a pattern matrix, starting from non-vanishing entries of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}
but also including higher-dimensional contributions from
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
. This definition separates the qualitative role of the pattern matrix from the quantitative information carried by the factor
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
, in particular by terms
\mathchar 28673_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785
, without
a priori
constraints on
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 381_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
.

We can now specify the notion of identifiability under partial information in our framework. We note that there is no loss of generality in considering the minor products (
1.3
) instead of the family of minors
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28673_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 381_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785
, as they let us reconstruct a canonical form for matrices
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}
and
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}
that is unique, up to the invariance (
1.4
), under the existence of indices satisfying a weaker condition than Assumption
10
(see Proposition
26
). This condition will be used as a means to explore the propagation of the integrability property between different bases (Section
6
). Then, Corollary
30
states that the trivial determinantal configurations (
1.5
) are the only ones returning constant
\mathchar 29017
-terms and, hence, preserving such a canonical form of the factor
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}
under monomial variations and Assumption
10
. Counterexamples can be found when such a property is not verified (see Section
7
). The generality of our discussion lets us take a generic covariance matrix and consider a partition of
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
into a set of
internal
labels, indexing the elements of a given
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
, and
external
labels in
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 28995}}
, in line with the terminology of
[
35
]
. In this way, we can focus on the
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785
-submatrix
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}
extracted from the original
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
-matrix by taking columns indexed by internal labels.

The choice of the form (
1.3
) is discriminant in the definition of valid deformations of this canonical form and, hence, of the minor ratios. We deepen this last point by examining the deformation
\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785
as a means to carry out a sensitivity analysis through monomial models in our factorisation problem. Monomial models are widely used in the probabilistic context, namely, graphical models; see
[
25
,
26
]
for more details in this regard. This algebraic parametrisation enables specific methods to conduct sensitivity analysis, i.e. the variation of model parameters and the study of their effects on a probability distribution
[
26
]
.
Adapting this notion to our investigation and the previous discussion, the objects of our sensitivity analysis are the independence structure
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
and, quantitatively, the canonical form of the factor based on
\mathchar 29017
-terms. While we assume
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 381_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
for generic
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}
, these objects undergo changes for a suitable choice of the evaluation point
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}
. This gives us the freedom to look at which
\mathchar 29017
-terms stay constant or vary (possibly vanish). In particular, for each family
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28998}\mathchar 12818\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
, we can also explore the simultaneous vanishing of the minors
\mathchar 28673_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28720}}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29000}\delimiter 84054785
,
\mathcal{\mathchar 29000}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28998}
, by looking at a function
\mathchar 28681
in (
3.1
) satisfying, for a given
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84267779
,
\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29000}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
at
\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mkern 1.0mu\delimiter 1299214\hfil$\crcr$\textstyle\mathchar 562$\crcr}}}\mathfrak{\mathchar 28998}
.

In principle, each minor
\mathchar 28673_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785
can be expressed as a monomial in an augmented set of variables
\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28996}
,
\mathchar 28996\mathchar 12606\mathchar 29028
; on the other hand, the Grassmann-Plücker relations generate a variable elimination process that restricts the valid expressions (
1.3
) for the remaining terms. While these determinantal relations always hold, the distinguished role of maximal minors in this work is taken into account by the deformation scheme (
1.3
) and, hence, deformations
\left\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785
that make the minor products (
2.6
)
reversible
, i.e. invertible in a given ring (here, the ring
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
of Laurent polynomials in the deformation variables
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}
). These deformations preserve the original information since the inversion
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}
returns the original minors. As we shall see (Section
4.2
), a central object to assess the compatibility of such algebraic expressions with determinantal conditions and the preservation of information provided by
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
(in line with Assumption
6
) is the
hyperdeterminant

[
20
]
derived from the combination of Grassmann-Plücker relations for
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
. The condition (
1.3
) makes this hyperdeterminant a sparse polynomial (we refer to
[
34
]
for more details in this regard).

As a final remark, it is worth mentioning that monomial parametrisations are also used in other graphical models; for example, the covariances in structural equation modelling can be expressed as the sum of monomials associated with treks in a graph that represents linear and confounding effects
[
39
]
.

3.2
Relation to choice theory

The second main application of the proposed models is the use of specific evaluations
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}
of the variables
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}
to rank the elements of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
based on the norm of the corresponding terms (
3.1
).

In this regard, the function
\mathchar 28681
in (
1.3
) can be seen as a (multidimensional at
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28721
) weighting function for a family of subsets of
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
. The counterexamples discussed in Section
7
and, more generally, the lack of a form (
3.2
) define a deviation from the additivity of the set function
\mathchar 28681
, which, in turn, can generate
incompatible
rankings for the base set
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
. Here, incompatibility refers to the violation of at least one axiom that describes rational choices or preferences
[
28
]
.

In particular,
for any point
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}
, the list of norms of minor products
\left\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 69645069\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 69645069\mathchar 12346\right\mathchar 314

\left\mathchar 314\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785
induces an order on
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
by pairwise comparison of the coordinates’ norms. So we can ask whether such a choice induces an order on the underlying index set
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
and, furthermore, if the two orders defined on
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
and
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
are consistent. Section
7.1
explores the limit behaviour of a sequence of evaluation points
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 29043}
,
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}
, and the corresponding order at
\delimiter 69645069\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 69645069\underset{{\scriptstyle\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561}}{\mathchar 12833}\mathchar 561
. This behaviour was investigated as a tropical limit for the study of metastability of statistical systems
[
5
]
; also see
[
2
]
for a focus on the order-theoretic aspects of inequivalence between tropical structures.
The results in Section
7.1
provide a natural algebraic construction showing a deviation from the Independence of Irrelevant Alternatives axiom, one of the most relevant properties characterising rational preferences
[
18
,
28
]
. The violation of this axiom is a prominent feature of
non-Bayesian
preference models where orders or rankings violate some of the implications of a Bayesian description of the preferences; see e.g.
[
1
]
and references therein. Ambiguity is a primary manifestation of this type of model uncertainty and is well represented by Ellsberg’s urn model
[
1
, Sect. 4]
, which in turn can be characterised by the non-additive behaviour of the subjective weights associated with events
[
18
, Sect. 3.4]
.

Weights defined by Plücker also give an alternative view of the factor indeterminacy discussed in the previous subsection, which stems from the existence of symmetry in the factor model that is not resolved by the observed variables
[
33
]
. The present geometric approach addresses this uncertainty with a natural parametrisation of a
subspace
in the space
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29038}
rather than individual components or directions, since such subspaces are invariant under
changes of basis of
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29035}
. Also in this regard, we can refer to (
3.2
) as a form of identifiability since it returns
\mathchar 29038
scaling factors
\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28939}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
,
\mathchar 28939\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
, each of which preserves the one-dimensional subspace generated by the corresponding row of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
.

In conclusion, we point out that deviations from the additivity of
\mathchar 28681
quantified by

\log\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}^{\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785
at
\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 29045}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721
,
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29028\delimiter 84267779
, deserve a deeper analysis in the context of Dempster-Shafer evidence theory (we refer to
[
15
, Sect. 2]
for more details on this theory and related notation). The normalisation of the weights
\log\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}^{\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785
returns the masses of a basic probability assignment (BPA) whose focal set (i.e. the class of elements of
\mathchar 381\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
with non-zero mass) is a subset of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
. Then, Theorem
11
provides us with minimal conditions on the family of potential focal sets that guarantee the existence of a well-defined
Bayesian
BPA (i.e. a BPA whose focal sets are the singletons in
\mathchar 381\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
) that is induced by weights
\log\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}^{\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\{\mathchar 28939\}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785
and whose corresponding belief function
\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathcal{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 12818\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}}\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785

[
15
, Sect. 2.2]
coincides with
\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785
,
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
, through an appropriate choice of the normalisation
\mathbf{\mathchar 29037}_{\mathchar 28720}
in (
3.2
). This suggests future investigation of the proposed approach in the context of geometric and combinatorial structures in evidence theory
[
15
]
, with particular attention to relative beliefs of singletons
[
14
]
.

4

Preliminary lemmas

The following lemmas are stated for future reference: they easily follow from direct computations, some of which are presented in

and are summarised here to make this work self-consistent.

Proposition 18
.

We have

Lemma 19
.

For

Theorem 23
.

Under Assumption
10
, we can find an integrable basis
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
.

Proof.

From Lemma

14
, the thesis is equivalent to the condition

\left\{\mathchar 29017\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 28942_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28942_{\mathchar 28722}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}}\mathchar 12346\,\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28942_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28942_{\mathchar 28722}}^{\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785\text{ is observable}\right\}\mathchar 12826\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}\mathchar 314

(5.3)

Taking into account Propositions
18
and
21
,
we only have to check observable sets
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28722}}^{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785
where at most one (upper or lower) index comes from a non-planar key
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29027}
. We label
\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 28722}
, and
\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 28722}

so that
\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28961_{\mathchar 29045}}^{\mathchar 29037_{\mathchar 29045}}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
,

6
Propagation of integrability between different bases

To extend the previous result to different bases, we select a weaker condition than Assumption
10
, which however suffices to ensure the propagation of the integrability property between bases. In this way, we take advantage of Theorem
23
, having a single structural property that guarantees the integrability of all the elements of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
. On the other hand, even when Assumption
10
does not hold, but
a priori
knowledge affirms the existence of an integrable basis, from the Theorem
25
stated below we are able to infer this property for other bases too.

Specifically, this propagation is entailed by the existence of a set
\{\mathchar 29031\}\mathchar 8706\{\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 28722}\}\mathchar 12818\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 8706\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 28995}}
, such that

\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 28721}}^{\mathchar 29031}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 28722}}^{\mathchar 29031}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314

(6.1)

Remark 24
.

The existence of two indices as in (
6.1
) holds for a set
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
if and only if it holds for any set
\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
, since the negation of this property entails that there exists a unique map
\mathchar 28965\mathchar 12346\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 28995}}\mathchar 12833\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
returning
\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28948}^{\mathchar 28965\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28948\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
. The possible changes of bases correspond to the substitution of a set
\mathcal{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 12818\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 29012}}
with
\mathchar 28965\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28993}\delimiter 84054785
, and this structure is preserved under such exchanges.

Also note that a dual argument holds, based on
[
4
, Rem. 2]
, regarding the existence of
\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
and
\mathchar 28948\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 28995}}
such that
\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28948}^{\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28948}^{\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28722}}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
. We will focus on (
6.1
) in the rest of this section, keeping in mind this duality.

Theorem 25
.

If there exists an integrable basis
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
and indices
\mathchar 29031\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
,
\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28948_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 28995}}
such that (
6.1
) holds, then each basis in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
is integrable. In particular, under Assumption
10
, each basis in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
is integrable.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
be integrable, and suppose, for the sake of contradiction, that the thesis does not hold for a given basis
\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
. We can construct a finite sequence
\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 24891\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 29042}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}
of elements of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
such that
\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\#\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 28673\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\delimiter 84054785
and
\#\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28673\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 29045}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
,
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29042\delimiter 84267779
as
stated in Lemma
12
. Say

\mathchar 29041\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\min\left\{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29042\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 12346\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\text{ is integrable and }\text{$\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}$}_{\mathchar 29045}\text{ is non-integrable}\right\}\mathchar 314

(6.2)

To simplify the notation, we denote
\mathcal{\mathchar 28993}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 29041\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
,
\mathcal{\mathchar 28994}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathcal{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 29041}
, and indices

such that

Definition (

) is equivalent to the existence of an observable set
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28994}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
that is not integrable, i.e.
\mathchar 29017\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 28994}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mkern 1.0mu\delimiter 1299214\hfil$\crcr$\textstyle\mathchar 562$\crcr}}}\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}
by Lemma

Instantiating (

(6.3)

we can move from terms of the form

So
\delimiter 69640972\mathrel{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mkern 1.0mu\delimiter 1299214\hfil$\crcr$\textstyle\mathchar 562$\crcr}}}\{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29034\}

Let us consider the family of minors

\displaystyle\mathchar 8235\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28939}^{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28939}^{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29016\mathchar 314

Here we show, for the sake of completeness, the steps to resolve the potential ambiguity in the assignment of the root of
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28941\mathchar 28942}^{\mathchar 29036\mathchar 29037}

Lemma 27
.

If all the bases in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
are integrable, for all
\mathchar 28939\triangledown\mathchar 28940

\mathchar 28960_{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28939\mathchar 24635\mathchar 28940\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28681\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}_{\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 28939}\delimiter 84054785\right\delimiter 84054785

(6.12)

\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}_{\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 28939}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
too. Specifically, this holds under the hypothesis of Theorem

, in particular when Assumption

is verified.

Proof.

\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 12346\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28721}

and

too, then define
\mathchar 28948\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\#\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 8814\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\delimiter 84054785

The proof proceeds by induction on
\mathchar 28948
. For the base step

, say

Remark 29
.

Lemma
27
involves fixed elements and a transition between bases, while the claim in the proof of Theorem
28
keeps the basis fixed and moves between the elements of
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
.
So these two problems can be seen as dual statements in terms of the bases (
6.24
) obtained from the Plücker relations.

Corollary 30
.

The trivial determinantal configurations (
1.5
) are the only ones returning constant
\mathchar 29017
-terms.

Proof.

It is immediately checked that (
1.5
) returns constant
\mathchar 29017
-terms. Vice versa, when all the
\mathchar 29017
-terms are constant, each basis in
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
is integrable by definition and Lemma
14
. Then, Lemma
27
and the proof of Theorem
28
hold and return the form (
1.5
).
∎

7
Counterexamples

The assumptions leading to the previous results highlight the required information content to recover integrability. In
[
4
]
, which pertains to cases with generic
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
, the amount of information is provided by the dimensionality of the matrices. Allowing for a larger sparsity of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
, as in this work, the information is provided by the existence of a non-planar key.

When the assumptions underlying the results in Section
6
are not met, we can find counterexamples to integrability: in the following, this is done by relaxing the individual conditions that define a non-planar key.

Where appropriate, in the rest of this section, we will use subscripts for block matrices to explicitly indicate the dimensions of the constituting blocks.

7.1
Reduction to Principal Minor Assignment

We start with an example that involves a matroid
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
that provides minimal information. Let us consider

\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29035}\mathchar 12906\mathbb{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8706\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785}

(7.1)

where
\mathbb{\mathchar 28721}_{\mathchar 29035}

\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 28673_{\mathbf{\tilde{\mathchar 29010}}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}_{\mathchar 28939}^{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28673_{\mathbf{\tilde{\mathchar 29010}}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\right\delimiter 0\mathchar 12604\mathchar 28721

since

is odd
and

is even.

We can say that the comparison of elements
\mathchar 29033
and
\mathchar 28939
exhibits contextuality, that is, it depends on the basis used for the comparison.
More precisely, we can interpret the norms
\delimiter 69645069\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 69645069^{\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785}
and
\delimiter 69645069\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 69645069^{\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785}
(or
\delimiter 69645069\mathchar 28673_{\tilde{\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 84054785}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 69645069
at
\delimiter 69645069\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28720}\delimiter 69645069\mathchar 12833\mathchar 561
) as weights that define two rankings on
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
and
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
, respectively. Then, a deviation from (
3.2
) leads to a violation of the Independence of Irrelevant Alternatives property, a fundamental condition in the study of decision-making and choice theory
[
28
, Chap. 1.C]
.

7.2

) and the generic condition,

, in contrast to (

).

8
Conclusion

In this work, we proposed a theoretical background to explore identifiability and dependence relations in factor models under partial information. In continuity with previous research
[
2
,
3
]
, we tested the effects of algebraic conditions combined with limited information on the characterisation of set functions (through additivity) and the identifiability of elements from a family of collections. We focused on a canonical form of a factor matrix to take advantage of a geometric representation, pointing out different connections to methods for the study of indeterminacy and relational structures in structural modelling (Subsection
3.1
).

Plücker coordinates are a natural way to encode information on such collections, highlighting observable ones through the matroid
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785
, and directly relate to data analysis under partial information on labels or object identity (e.g. in the context of unlabelled sensing
[
38
]
). In Subsection
3.2
, we also pointed out potential connections with other geometric methods
[
15
]
to represent uncertainty; the possible implications for representations of belief functions, random sets, and multi-valued mapping deserve a dedicated analysis.

In this regard, future work will also explore related applications of this approach in describing ambiguity. Indeed, this type of uncertainty falls within the scope of identifiability and labelling of elements in distinct collections, as is the case for the paradigmatic Ellsberg urn models, where different colour labellings and, hence, probabilistic descriptions interfere and result in deviations from a classical (Bayesian) description.

In conclusion, a detailed study of the principal minor assignment problem is needed to characterise non-integrable configurations for a better comprehension of the minimality of Assumption
10
beyond the examples presented in Section
7
. A focus on factor multiplicity for hyperdeterminants (
4.14
) and their products under different algebraic forms besides (
1.3
) can support the extension of this investigation to other types of deformations of dependence structures.
